July 7, 2026 08:00 PM GMT

Electronic Components | Japan

# Apr-Jun Q Earnings Outlook: Sustainability of Strong Earnings the Key Consideration

We forecast total Apr-Jun OP for 19 firms will increase 33.6% YoY, with strong demand from AI/data center applications continuing, but rising raw material costs warrant close attention.

## Key Takeaways

We expect continued growth for ABF package substrates for AI/DC semis and MLCCs; rising raw materials cost a key risk for aluminum electrolytic capacitors.

Murata Mfg. still our Top Pick, continue to rate Taiyo Yuden UW. We expect the strength of Murata's high-value-added MLCCs to become increasingly evident.

We also expect Ibiden's earnings expansion to continue over the longer term, but remain UW as market expectations and valuation look too high.

Expect strong Apr-Jun results for beneficiaries of AI/DC-related sales growth, inc. Ibiden & Murata: For Ibiden, we expect strong growth in sales of high-value-added ABF package substrates, with sales for NVIDIA's Rubin platform exceeding those for Blackwell. With use of the declining-balance method, we estimate 1Q depreciation expense will decline by ¥4.5bn QoQ, enabling high earnings from 1Q. For Murata, we expect high earnings from 1Q driven by rising sales of high-value-added MLCCs for AI/DC applications and higher utilization rates in core MLCCs. OW on Murata, TDK, Hirose Electric, Niterra, Alps Alpine: Murata's longer-term earnings growth is driven by an improving mix on rising sales of high-value-added MLCCs for AI/DC use. For TDK, profit growth from rechargeable batteries is limited in F3/27, but expect HDD heads and passive components to drive growth, with rechargeable batteries for DC BBUs to contribute from F3/29. For Hirose, we expect sales growth in connectors for both industrial equipment and AI servers, and continued ROE improvement longer term via enhanced shareholder returns and strategic investments. For Niterra, we expect stable, high level profit from spark plugs to increase gradually, market share gains in exhaust gas oxygen sensors, and continued sales growth of electrostatic chucks for SPE customers in Japan/US. UW on Taiyo Yuden, Ibiden, Nippon Chemi-Con, Daishinku: Taiyo Yuden's core MLCCs should continue expanding, but we see the earnings gap vs. Murata (higher weighting of high-value-added MLCCs for AI/DC) widening, and valuation is rich. Ibiden's near-term earnings are strong, but bar to achieving its F3/31 OP target of ¥300bn is high (if achieved, F3/31 P/E still a lofty \~24x). Chemi-Con has risk to 2H F3/27 OP guidance (¥5bn) and high valuation (\~50x F3/27e EPS for common shares, post dilution & preferred divs). For Daishinku, we're cautious on risk of delayed contributions from crystal oscillators for optical transceivers or failure to gain meaningful share, slowing demand for crystal devices in Chinese smartphones/PCs.

## Apr-Jun results preview

Ibiden (4062.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥21.6bn (FactSet consensus: ¥20.2bn). We anticipate +¥4.1bn QoQ growth, mainly because we expect Electronics sales to rise by +¥4.8bn, while we look for companywide depreciation to decline by -¥4.5bn.

Niterra (5334.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥39.6bn (FactSet consensus: ¥30.7bn), +¥12.3bn QoQ. Key factors include the non-recurrence of several items from 4Q, including \~¥12bn in inventory valuation losses and structural reform costs, \~¥3bn in PPA expenses related to silicon nitride, \~¥4bn in gains on the sale of land at the former headquarters, and \~¥1.5bn in fixed-asset tax in Japan.

Minebea Mitsumi (6479.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥26.0bn (FactSet consensus: ¥27.4bn), -¥2.3bn QoQ. While the MLS segment is likely to see profit growth driven by higher HDD spindle motor volumes, we expect slight profit declines in PT (mainly miniature ball bearings) and SE (mainly semiconductors, camera actuators, game assemblies), as well as a drop in Access Solutions (AS; mainly automotive components) due to factors such as rising material costs.

Mabuchi Motor (6592.T): We forecast F12/26 2Q OP of ¥7.1bn (FactSet consensus: ¥6.4bn), +¥1.7bn QoQ. We expect motor production volume to rise from 313mn units in 1Q to 330mn in 2Q. We also expect motor shipment volumes to increase QoQ in both the Automotive Products and Life & Industrial Products segments.

TDK (6762.T): For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP +¥22.8bn QoQ to ¥64.5bn (FactSet consensus: ¥64.6bn) mainly due to the absence of ¥9.8bn in restructuring costs recorded in 4Q and sharp growth in sales of HDD heads for STX and WDC, which resumed in 4Q.

Alps Alpine (6770.T): For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP -¥5.1bn QoQ to ¥3.7bn (FactSet consensus: ¥5.3bn), assuming Component OP increases on growth in sales of new high added-value smartphone camera actuators, but Mobility profit declines ¥9.4bn QoQ to a ¥3bn loss from a sharp decline in development fees collected from customers.

Nippon Dempa Kogyo (6779.T): For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP to decrease slightly to ¥1.1bn. While we forecast modest QoQ growth in sales, we assume a hit of \~¥0.1bn from higher R&D costs.

Meiko Electronics (6787.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q OP +¥1bn QoQ to ¥8.1bn (FactSet consensus: ¥8.2bn). We assume profit gains will primarily be driven by growth in sales of build-up substrates for satellite communications, the pass-through of raw material cost increases last 4Q, and higher operating rates.

Hirose Electric (6806.T): Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥11.3bn (+¥0.8bn QoQ; FS consensus ¥11.5bn). Despite negative impact from rising precious metal prices QoQ, we expect profit growth due to increased sales centered on general industrial equipment and communications infrastructure, as well as the absence of approximately ¥0.5bn in one-off costs recorded in 4Q of the previous year.

Japan Aviation Electronics (6807.T): Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥0.6bn (-¥2.4bn QoQ; FS consensus ¥2.8bn). The main drivers of the significant QoQ decline are higher raw material costs due to surging gold prices, the absence of more than ¥1.0bn in positive effects from pricing optimization for specific customers recorded in 4Q of the previous year, and seasonal declines in aerospace-related sales.

CMK (6958.T): Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥0.7bn (–¥0.8bn QoQ; FS consensus ¥0.3bn). We expect the main drivers of the QoQ decline to be seasonal reductions in sales to Japanese Tier 1 customers and impact from the Lunar New Year holiday at overseas plants operating on a different schedule.

Daishinku (6962.T): Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥0.3bn (−¥0.3bn QoQ). Despite QoQ revenue growth, we expect OP to decline sequentially due to higher depreciation and rising raw material costs.

Hamamatsu Photonics (6965.T): We forecast F9/26 3Q OP to decline ¥2.7bn QoQ, from ¥7.6bn to ¥4.9bn (consensus ¥5.3bn). Hamamatsu typically sees seasonal declines in 1Q and 3Q and increases in 2Q and 4Q, and so we expect a QoQ decline in 3Q. However, earnings are on a recovery trend, with OP expected to jump from ¥1.5bn in F9/25 3Q and ¥2.4bn in F9/26 1Q.

Kyocera (6971.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q OP down ¥18.6bn QoQ to ¥28.9bn (FS consensus ¥27.4bn). The sharp QoQ decline in Solutions reflects the absence of roughly ¥17.0bn in disposal gains in the prior quarter and seasonally weaker document-related sales, which typically peak in 4Q and decline in 1Q.

Taiyo Yuden (6976.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q OP at ¥7.0bn (consensus ¥7.3bn), up ¥3.5bn QoQ from ¥3.5bn last 4Q. MLCC utilization is likely to rise to 90% from just under 85% in 4Q, supporting top-line growth.

Murata Manufacturing (6981.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥90.4bn (FS: ¥85.6bn), up by ¥11.5bn QoQ with components profit increasing by ¥10.8bn QoQ to ¥91.9bn, and devices/modules up by ¥0.4bn QoQ to breakeven. We expect components to see continuing growth in sales of MLCCs for AI/data centers as well as ongoing improvement in the MLCC product mix and growth in capacity utilization, and devices modules continuing improvement in battery earnings and bottoming out of sales for North American smartphones, despite non-reoccurrence of the ¥6.0bn gain on the sale of micro-battery business.

Nichicon (6996.T): For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP down ¥0.9bn QoQ at ¥1.7bn (FS: ¥1.5bn), including falls each for capacitors and NECST owing to seasonally-driven sales contraction.

Nippon Chemi-Con (6997.T): We forecast F3/27 1Q of ¥1.2bn, down ¥0.2bn QoQ in a modest fall reflecting lower sales of all products apart from AECs and rising costs for raw materials such as aluminum foil.

KOA (6999.T): For F3/27 1Q we forecast OP +¥0.1bn QoQ to ¥0.6bn (FS: ¥0.5bn), on the view that sales will stay flat QoQ but positive effects of reduced depreciation should cancel out the negative of rising raw material costs.

# MLCCs: Expect differing product clout to produce longer-term earnings variations

## MLCCs are the most important products for Murata and Taiyo Yuden

In F3/26, MLCCs made up 51% of sales at Murata and 71% of sales at Taiyo Yuden, and we expect them to drive earnings growth at both companies from F3/27. Though earnings growth at both is outpacing the market consensus, of the two the shares of Taiyo Yuden have outperformed in 2026 YTD (up to July 6), advancing by 419% vs. Murata's +216%. This leaves Taiyo Yuden looking overvalued at P/E of 37.3x on our F3/31e diluted EPS, vs. Murata on 21.1x. While growth in sales of MLCCs for AI/data centers stands to lift earnings at both companies, we see limited benefits of product mix improvement due to MLCC miniaturization and capacitance increases for Taiyo Yuden, and downgraded the stock to UW on June 17 on the view that the gap between the share price and the earnings outlook up to F3/31 has widened (report link).

## Feedback from Top Pick on Murata, UW downgrade on Taiyo Yuden

Publication of our report on June 17 provided an opportunity to discuss the near- and longer-term outlook for the MLCC market with many market participants. Three key tenets of our report were that 1) among capacitors that are less likely to be integrated into semiconductor circuits, demand for MLCCs will increase in the longer term; 2) Murata will maintain its #1 position supported by its competitiveness not only in technology but also in costs; and 3) there is a lot of scope for downside in Taiyo Yuden shares, which have risen to trade on higher multiples than Murata, and look overvalued. Despite cyclical swings, we expect MLCC demand to continue expanding in the medium and longer term, and Murata to maintain the #1 position, and we found many who agreed with our view. This is also the case for our view that Taiyo Yuden is overvalued, but some investors have also opined that Murata shares could fall as well in a phase of decline in Taiyo Yuden's share price.

## Expect small, high-capacitance value-added MLCCs for AI/DCs to drive the MLCC market

We estimate global MLCC shipment value at US\$14.67bn (+10.3% YoY) in 2025, and that shares were 40.8% for Murata, 22.5% for SEMCO, 11.3% for Taiyo Yuden, 6.9% for TDK, 5.4% for Yageo, and 13.0% collectively for makers other than the top 5. This implies a 10-year CAGR of +6.5% for MLCC global shipment value, from \$7.8bn in 2025, but for the coming three years we foresee a faster CAGR of +18.2%, to \$24.25bn in 2028. We expect rising sales of small, high-capacitance value-added products for AI servers and data centers to have a considerable impact in expanding MLCC demand, and see Murata as the likely main beneficiary due to its ability to supply small, high-capacitance units in large quantities before competitors.

## Murata leads the way in small, high-capacitance MLCCs for AI servers

Murata has been a pioneer in MLCC miniaturization and higher capacitance, having started shipping mass-produced 1005-size 1μF MLCCs in 2000, and 0402-size 1μF units as well as 1005-size 22μF units in 2020, among other milestones. Demand for 1608-size 100 F, 1005-size 47μF and 0603-size 10μF MLCCs for AI servers is rising sharply, and Murata appears to be the only company capable of securing stable yields in mass production of all of these.

## Expect differences in MLCC product clout to open up mid/longer-term earnings gaps

There are many Japanese electronic component companies, such as Murata in MLCCs, with the ability to compete in both technology and costs. Many of them are not moving quickly to raise prices on the grounds of tight supply/demand, and many of the latter are enjoying high levels of profit backed by their strong technology competitiveness in cutting-edge components. At the same time they are also maintaining high cost competitiveness in general-purpose products. We believe the general-purposes space in MLCCs has many participants, including Chinese companies, and that distributors are often involved in transactions. We further infer that prices for MLCCs charged by distributors to customers and by MLCCs makers to distributors are heavily influenced by the S/D situation at the time. We also think it likely that Murata adjusts its prices for general-purpose MLCCs to distributors flexibly, both upwards and downwards, in response to market prices. At the same time, while increases in general-purpose MLCC prices can help Murata too to maximize near-term profit, there is also a risk of them leading to a lower share for the company in the mid/longer term as entry barriers come down enabling more participation by companies from China, South Korea, Taiwan and elsewhere. For such companies, rising market prices for general-purpose products make it possible to book profit where this has not consistently been the case, positioning them better to develop and enter the markets of not just commodity but also middle- and high-end products. We understand that distributors and MLCC makers who mainly handle commodity products and are seeking to maximize near-term profit in the general-purposes MLCC market are currently warning customers about risks of future MLCC shortages and price increases in a bid to push up prices for general-purpose products. Nevertheless, we expect expansion of the scale of the MLCC market to be heavily influenced by rising average selling prices stemming from growth in volumes of value-added products, such as small, high-capacitance units for AI/DCs, and in other products with higher added-value. As such, taking a longer-term view of the MLCC market, we think companies such as Murata, with superior abilities to compete in both technology and costs, stand to see significantly more growth in corporate value than those who aim to maximize near-term profit via short-term increases in prices of general-purpose MLCCs.

## We forecast earnings assuming ¥155/\$ and ¥180/€

Our forecasts for Apr-Jun and beyond are based on our forex assumptions of ¥155/\$ and ¥180/€. Based on each company's briefing, we estimate that a ¥1/\$ fluctuation would result in an impact of ¥14.9bn (1.0% of FY26e OP) in the total annual OP of our coverage universe. For companies with fiscal years ending in March, the average ¥/\$ exchange rate was ¥112.3/\$ in FY21, ¥135.4/\$ in FY22, ¥144.5/\$ in FY23, ¥152.5/\$ in FY24, and ¥150.6/\$ in FY25. For companies such as Murata, Hirose, Niterra that produce a high percentage of their core products in Japan and have a high percentage of sales denominated in foreign currencies, we expect a weaker yen/stronger dollar to boost profits and a stronger yen/weaker dollar to depress profits. In our view, the impact of exchange rates on the earnings of companies with a high ratio of overseas sales of module components, motors, and others is often unknown until the earnings of overseas subsidiaries is consolidated with that of the Japanese headquarters.

## We expect combined OP for our coverage universe to rise 31% YoY in FY26 and rise 34% in Apr-Jun 2026

We forecast combined OP for our coverage universe to reach ¥1.5419trn in FY26 (FactSet consensus: ¥1.4912trn), up ¥365.7bn/31.1% from ¥1.1762trn in FY25. After OP declined for two consecutive years, from ¥1,261.5bn in FY21 (+58.0% YoY) to ¥1,098.2bn in FY22 (−12.9%) and ¥879.5bn in FY23 (−19.9%), it turned to growth in FY24 at ¥984.5bn (+11.9%) and ¥1,176.2bn in FY25 (+18.2%), and we expect the pace of earnings growth to accelerate in FY26.

We forecast combined OP for our coverage companies in Apr–Jun at ¥319.2bn, up 33.6% from ¥238.9bn in Apr–Jun 2025 and up 8.3% from ¥294.8bn in Jan–Mar 2026. OP at major electronic component companies typically peaks in Jul-Sep, declines in Oct-Dec, and declines further in Jan-Mar. However, we believe the QoQ decline in Jan-Mar 2026 will be smaller than usual, reflecting strong demand related to AI servers and data centers, as well as one-off expenses of ¥49.8bn by Murata in Oct-Dec, including goodwill impairment related to Resonant.

# Key points underlying our earnings outlook

## Ibiden (4062, UW, PT ¥13,000)

We forecast Ibiden's F3/27 OP at ¥94.7bn (.6 FactSet consensus: ¥99.7bn). Our estimate of +¥32.7bn YoY growth mainly reflects an expected +¥32.7bn increase in earnings from the Electronics business. We expect this sharp increase in Electronics earnings to be driven primarily by higher sales of ABF package substrates for NVIDIA, as NVIDIA's flagship products shift from Blackwell to Rubin, with an accompanying rise in unit prices. We estimate that the unit price of ABF package substrates for Rubin will be roughly twice that of Blackwell.

We forecast F3/28 OP of ¥128.5bn (company guidance: ¥150bn; FactSet consensus: ¥151.5bn). The implied ¥33.7bn YoY increase is again mainly due to an expected ¥32.1bn increase in earnings from Electronics. In Electronics, we expect NVIDIA's AI server GPUs to shift from Rubin to Rubin Ultra, supporting a continued rise in the average selling price of ABF package substrates. In addition, against the backdrop of increased procurement of T-Glass/CCL and higher production volumes at the Ohno Plant, we forecast that ABF package substrates sales will rise +31% YoY, or ¥100.8bn. Although we expect depreciation to increase by +¥15bn, from ¥71bn in FY3/27 to ¥86bn in F3/28, we believe Ibiden can still secure profit growth, although we do not expect OP to reach company guidance.

We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥21.6bn (FactSet consensus: ¥20.2bn). We anticipate +¥4.1bn QoQ growth, mainly because we expect Electronics sales to rise by +¥4.8bn, while we look for companywide depreciation to decline by -¥4.5bn.

## Niterra (5334, OW, PT ¥12,600)

For Niterra, we forecast F3/27 OP of ¥158.2bn (company guidance: ¥150bn; FactSet consensus: ¥154.0bn). The breakdown of our projected +¥20bn YoY increase includes +¥13.8bn Automotive Components earnings and +¥13.5bn in Component Solutions earnings. In Automotive Components, we expect profits to increase as the benefit of higher prices for plugs sold in the replacement market continues. We also expect profit growth for exhaust gas sensors driven by higher sales of oxygen sensors for motorcycles.

For Component Solutions, we look for growth not only from increased earnings in electrostatic chucks for semiconductor production equipment (SPE), but also from reduced in losses in medical business.

We forecast F3/28 OP of ¥174.4bn (FactSet consensus: ¥174.9bn), implying +¥16.2bn YoY growth. This consists mainly of a +¥12.3bn increase in Automotive Components earnings and a +¥3.9bn increase in Component Solutions. We expect not only continued earnings growth from replacement market plugs, but also gradual increases in sales of plugs for new vehicles and exhaust gas sensors. While we believe the transfer of spark plug and exhaust oxygen sensor businesses from Denso will take time, we expect Niterra will continue gradually gaining market share in both of these product areas.

We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥39.6bn (FactSet consensus: ¥30.7bn), +¥12.3bn QoQ. Key factors include the non-recurrence of several items from 4Q, including \~¥12bn in inventory valuation losses and structural reform costs, \~¥3bn in PPA expenses related to silicon nitride, \~¥4bn in gains on the sale of land at the former headquarters, and \~¥1.5bn in fixed-asset tax in Japan.

## Minebea Mitsumi (6479, EW, PT ¥5,300)

We forecast F3/27 OP of ¥121.5bn for Minebea Mitsumi (company guidance: ¥120bn; FactSet consensus: ¥121.3bn), +¥17.5bn YoY on expected profit growth across all four segments. In Precision Technologies (PT), where the core products are miniature ball bearings, strong demand for these products is likely to continue, including for DC fan motors. In Motor, Lighting & Sensing (MLS), which is centered on motors, we expect solid sales of spindle motors for HDDs along with higher sales of automotive motors.

We forecast F3/28 OP of ¥137.8bn (FactSet consensus: ¥136.0bn), +¥16.3bn YoY. In addition to continued growth in ball bearing and motor sales, we expect profit growth to be supported by rising sales of high-value-added smartphone camera actuators in the Semiconductor & Electronics (SE) segment, as production ramps up smoothly. That said, we expect competitor Alps Alpine to be the principal vendor for smartphone camera actuators in North America, so the extent of the benefit from higher-value-added products at Minebea Mitsumi uncertain.

We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥26.0bn (FactSet consensus: ¥26.3bn), -¥2.3bn QoQ. While the MLS segment is likely to see profit growth driven by higher HDD spindle motor

volumes, we expect slight profit declines in PT (mainly miniature ball bearings) and SE (mainly semiconductors, camera actuators, game assemblies), as well as a drop in Access Solutions (AS; mainly automotive components) due to factors such as rising material costs.

## Mabuchi Motor (6592, EW, PT ¥1,750)

For Mabuchi Motor, we forecast F12/26 OP of ¥27.2bn (company guidance: ¥26bn; FactSet consensus: ¥24.7bn). Although we expect labour costs will continue to rise, we do not anticipate a major change in total personnel expenses due to labour-saving measures. Comparing the earnings improvement from structural reforms in Poland and elsewhere, we believe enhanced productivity from automation efforts and other measures and restructuring benefits will outweigh the impact of rising costs such as wages, leading us to forecast +¥1.8bn YoY growth in OP.

We forecast F12/27 OP of ¥29.9bn (FactSet consensus: ¥26.8bn). We expect profit growth to continue, supported by higher production and shipment volumes of automotive motors, market share gains in motors for power windows and power seats, and productivity improvements at acquired companies.

We forecast F12/26 2Q OP of ¥7.1bn (FactSet consensus: ¥6.8bn), +¥1.7bn QoQ. We expect motor production volume to rise from 313mn units in 1Q to 330mn in 2Q. We also expect motor shipment volumes to increase QoQ in both the Automotive Products and Life & Industrial Products segments.

## TDK (6762, OW, PT ¥4,900)

We forecast F3/27 OP +¥63.3bn YoY to ¥335.8bn (guidance: ¥295bn, FactSet consensus: ¥307.7bn). By segment, we forecast OP +¥12.2bn to ¥54bn in Passive Components, with growth in sales of core products, including MLCCs, aluminum electrolytic capacitors and inductors, for not only automotive, but also AI/DC applications; +¥1.5bn to ¥22.2bn for Sensor Application Products, on sustained profitability in MEMS sensors and improved profitability in temperature and pressure sensors; +¥34.5bn to ¥61.5bn in Magnetic Application Products, driven mainly by increased sales of HDD heads and suspensions; and +¥9.4bn to ¥256.1bn in Energy Application Products, with growth in sales of high added-value rechargeable batteries and the drop-out of ¥7.4bn in power supply restructuring costs. For F3/28, we forecast OP +33.4bn YoY to ¥369.1bn (FactSet consensus: ¥355.7bn), assuming growth across all four segments, with the largest drivers as increased sales and productivity for MLCCs, aluminum electrolytic capacitors and inductors in Passive Components, and continued profitability gains on MEMS sensors in Sensor Application Products.

For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP +¥22.8bn QoQ to ¥64.5bn (FactSet consensus: ¥64.6bn) mainly due to the absence of ¥9.8bn in restructuring costs recorded in 4Q and sharp growth in sales of HDD heads for STX and WDC, which resumed in 4Q.

![](images/cd9bc299cf8ea153122436bd03b6496e7ff1b9515535a2d950dc2335cf5c0f5c.jpg)

We forecast F3/27 OP +¥7.1bn YoY to ¥49.1bn (guidance: ¥48.5bn, FactSet consensus: ¥43.4bn). By segment, we forecast OP +¥6.9bn to ¥37.1bn in Component, with sharp profit gains in smartphone camera actuators making up for lower profit in smartphone and game switches; at -¥1.2bn in Sensor & Communication, assuming the booking of sales on digital keys and lower development costs serve to narrow losses, but that a turn to profit remains out of grasp; and -¥2bn to ¥12.2bn in Mobility, with lower sales due to new project delays and other factors outweighing the continued receipt of development fees from customers. For F3/28, we forecast OP +¥19.6bn YoY to ¥68.7bn (FactSet consensus: ¥49.8bn), assuming Component OP grows ¥14.6bn with the stabilized production of high added-value camera actuators for North American smartphones, Sensor & Communication losses turn to a ¥2bn profit, and Mobility OP increases ¥1.8bn due to the effect of consolidating development and production sites.

For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP -¥5.1bn QoQ to ¥3.7bn (FactSet consensus: ¥5.3bn), assuming Component OP increases on growth in sales of new high added-value smartphone camera actuators, but Mobility profit declines ¥9.4bn QoQ to a ¥3bn loss from a sharp decline in development fees collected from customers.

## Nihon Dempa Kogyo (6779, EW, PT ¥4,900)

We forecast F3/27 OP +¥1.2bn YoY to ¥4.6bn (guidance: ¥4bn), assuming sales growth momentum picks up to +13% YoY for sales to hit ¥61.9bn (guidance ¥60.6bn). By application, we expect sales for Industrial Equipment to rise 1.9x on increased demand for optical transceiver and AI/DC internal products and drive overall earnings. We also expect a contribution from growth in sales for Automotive applications through 1H, backed by moves to secure safety stock amid the soaring cost of memory and other components. While we assume in-house IC development costs and costs associated with proactive upfront investments (DX, labor, etc.) will increase, we believe marginal profit growth will exceed this, resulting in YoY profit gains that exceed guidance.

For F3/28, we forecast OP +¥0.7bn YoY to ¥5.3bn (company forecast in current mid-term plan: ¥7bn). While the bar to achieving the mid-term plan remains high, we maintain the view for sustained growth in sales of crystal oscillators for optical transceivers and continued earnings growth in products linked to business portfolio diversification, such as optical components for SPE and communication equipment for defense. If utilization of highly efficient production lines ramps up fully, we would expect the company to enter a

Note: Consensus is based on FactSet; Source: FactSet

recovery phase for ongoing upfront investment costs. We also eye the launch timing of MEMS substitutes for North American high-end smartphones and the possibility of recapturing market share.

For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP to decrease slightly to ¥1.1bn. While we forecast modest QoQ growth in sales, we assume a hit of \~¥0.1bn from higher R&D costs.

## Meiko (6787, EW, PT ¥39,200)

We forecast F3/27 OP +¥14bn YoY to ¥38.6bn (guidance: ¥38bn, FactSet consensus: ¥38.1bn). We assume upside to guidance will be driven by ales for AI server applications growing +¥12.7bn to ¥20bn (guidance: ¥17bn) on increased sales mainly of eSSD module substrates to existing customers and the start of mass production for new customers. Mass production of AI accelerator boards is due to commence from 2H, and we assume this will contribute sales of \~¥7bn. In satellite communication applications, we forecast sales to rise a sharp 2.0x to ¥56bn (guidance: ¥55bn) on rapid growth in demand for receiving antenna substrates owing to service expansion to other regions. We also expect OPM to widen to 11.9%, level with guidance, on a better mix resulting from an increased sales contribution from high-layer count, high-density build-up substrates and progress in passing through CCL and other raw material costs.

For F3/28, we forecast OP +¥10.1bn YoY to ¥48.7bn (guidance: ¥51bn; FactSet consensus: ¥51.3bn), assuming build-up substrate sales grow a strong +45%, backed by the start of mass production of satellite main body substrates, expansion of the customer base for AI accelerator boards, and the ramp up of mass production of products for North American high-end smartphones. On the other hand, we expect depreciation and amortization expenses to rise a sharp +¥17.3bn to ¥34.8bn based on the company's plans to sustain annual capex of ¥70bn over F3/27-29. Key in our view will be whether a better mix and further top-line growth can offset the continued rise in raw material costs and higher fixed costs associated with the start-up of new plants.

We forecast F3/27 1Q OP +¥1bn QoQ to ¥8.1bn (FactSet consensus: ¥8.2bn. We assume profit gains will primarily be driven by growth in sales of build-up substrates for satellite communications, the pass-through of raw material cost increases last 4Q, and higher operating rates.

## Hirose Electric (6806, OW, PT ¥36,600)

Our F3/27 OP forecast is ¥47.3bn (+¥4.3bn YoY; company forecast ¥46.0bn, FS consensus ¥49.8bn). We forecast sales for general industrial equipment applications at ¥73.9bn (+¥11.9bn YoY; company forecast ¥71.0bn), exceeding the company plan and maintaining a high pace of revenue growth. We expect Hirose to benefit from increased investment in production equipment, particularly for SPE-related applications. For communications infrastructure, where demand for mezzanine connectors for AI servers is expanding rapidly, we forecast sales of ¥10.7bn (company forecast ¥9.0bn). Revenue growth from SER (acquired in F3/26; main products are probes and probe-applied products for SPE) should also contribute. While we expect the impact of higher raw material costs due to rising precious metal prices to expand YoY, we believe profit growth exceeding guidance is achievable, driven by higher marginal profit and progress in price pass-through, particularly for smartphones and industrial equipment. We also expect that the significant price reductions for high-end smartphones in North America seen in the previous year will not recur in F3/27.

Our F3/28 OP forecast is ¥53.4bn (+¥6.1bn YoY; FS consensus ¥58.1bn). Our view remains unchanged that historically high revenue growth will continue, and we expect further growth in B-to-B connector sales for AI servers. Price pass-through for rising precious metal prices should also progress gradually. The company has announced a midterm management plan with F3/30 as the final year, and we view all company KPIs (revenue ¥290bn, OP ¥66.7bn, ROE 12%) as achievable.

Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥11.3bn (+¥0.8bn QoQ; FS consensus ¥11.5bn). Despite negative impact from rising precious metal prices QoQ, we expect profit growth due to increased sales centered on general industrial equipment and communications infrastructure, as well as the absence of approximately ¥0.5bn in one-off costs recorded in 4Q of the previous year.

## Japan Aviation Electronics Industry (6807, EW, PT ¥2,600)

Our F3/27 OP forecast is ¥8.9bn (flat YoY; company forecast ¥9.5bn, FS consensus ¥13.2bn). We forecast connector sales at ¥212.1bn (+¥5.2bn YoY; company forecast ¥215.0bn), implying a slight increase YoY but somewhat below the company plan. For automotive, we expect solid demand growth for ADAS-related wire harnesses, but we forecast a YoY decline in mobile device-related sales, including Type-C connectors and connectors for Chinese smartphones. For aerospace, we forecast sales of ¥22.5bn (+¥2.0bn YoY; company forecast ¥24.5bn), also below the company plan as with connectors, although we expect recovery in high-margin commercial demand such as sensors for oil drilling and SPE applications. We forecast OP to remain flat YoY due to higher raw material costs from rising precious metal prices and increased depreciation.

Our F3/28 OP forecast is ¥9.6bn (+¥0.7bn YoY; FS consensus ¥16.1bn). Our view remains that the pace of earnings recovery will be moderate relative to market consensus and the current midterm plan targets. In addition, Kyocera announced on Oct 30, 2025, that it had acquired a 33.0% stake in JAE held by NEC (6701.T, covered by Tetsuro Tsusaka). Through this capital alliance with Kyocera, JAE expects specific synergies such as market share gains via joint development of smartphone connectors and cross-selling of connectors for automotive and industrial equipment. However, the possibility of additional share acquisition by Kyocera remains a key point to watch. Kyocera's acquisition price of ¥3,630 per share on Oct 30, 2025 exceeded the Oct 29 closing price of ¥2,779 by 30.6% and is 39.6% higher than our price target (base case fair value) of ¥2,600.

Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥0.6bn (-¥2.4bn QoQ; FS consensus ¥2.8bn). The main drivers of the significant QoQ decline are higher raw material costs due to surging gold prices, the absence of more than ¥1.0bn in positive effects from pricing optimization for specific customers recorded in 4Q of the previous year, and seasonal declines in aerospace-related sales.

## CMK (6958, EW, PT ¥790)

Our F3/27 OP forecast is ¥3.2bn (+¥0.4bn YoY; company forecast ¥3.2bn, FS consensus ¥3.5bn). We forecast build-up PCB sales at ¥37.2bn (+¥4.2bn YoY; company forecast ¥37.3bn). For Japanese Tier 1 customers, we expect performance to remain firm supported by continued demand growth in integrated ECU applications, while for European Tier 1 customers, we assume a gradual demand recovery with F3/26 as the bottom. On the other hand, we forecast double-sided and multilayer through-hole PCB sales at ¥62.7bn (–¥0.7bn YoY; company forecast ¥62.9bn). Our outlook reflects accelerated customer shifts away from Greater China supply chains and tight supply–demand conditions for raw materials such as CCL. For OP, we expect profit growth broadly in line with the company's guidance, as improvements in product mix and positive effects from resolving production system issues at the new Thailand plant last year more than offset rising raw material costs and increased depreciation.

Our F3/28 OP forecast is ¥3.9bn (+¥0.7bn YoY; FS consensus ¥5.0bn). We expect gradual earnings expansion relative to the 3-year mid-term plan KPIs announced at F3/26 full-year results (F3/29 revenue ¥120bn, OP ¥7.5bn). Key focus areas will continue to be the outlook for build-up PCB sales for integrated ECUs, project wins in non-automotive areas such as SPE and infrastructure, and the pace of profitability improvement at the new Thailand plant.

Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥0.7bn (−¥0.8bn QoQ; FS consensus ¥0.3bn). We expect the main drivers of the QoQ decline to be seasonal reductions in sales to Japanese Tier 1 customers and impact from the Lunar New Year holiday at overseas plants operating on a different schedule.

## Daishinku (6962, UW, PT ¥840)

Our F3/27 OP forecast is ¥1.0bn (–¥0.1bn YoY; company forecast ¥1.4bn, FS consensus ¥1.4bn). We forecast communications sales at ¥10.7bn (+¥0.5bn YoY; company forecast ¥11.1bn), falling short of the company plan due to reduced shipments of mid- to low-end Chinese smartphones. We expect OP to decline YoY as the positive effects of revenue growth and price pass-through are offset by approximately ¥1.0bn in higher raw material costs from rising gold prices, as well as increases in fixed costs such as depreciation and R&D.

Our F3/28 OP forecast is ¥1.5bn (+¥0.5bn YoY; FS consensus ¥2.0bn). It is difficult to forecast the contribution from sales expansion into 1.6T optical transceivers via the company's proprietary Arkh. series, and we think it will take time for earnings contributions to fully materialize. While the share price has recently risen significantly on expectations of rapid demand growth for AI/data center timing devices, including optical transceivers, we assume the company's market share gains will be limited in the short term. Labor shortages at production sites could also become a bottleneck in scaling up capacity during periods of increasing demand.

Our F3/27 1Q OP forecast is ¥0.3bn (−¥0.3bn QoQ). Despite QoQ revenue growth, we expect OP to decline sequentially due to higher depreciation and rising raw material costs.

## Hamamatsu Photonics (6965, EW, PT ¥3,000)

We forecast F9/26 OP up ¥6.3bn YoY to ¥22.5bn (company guidance ¥20.0bn, FactSet consensus ¥20.1bn). This reflects a recovery in industrial equipment sales, including semiconductor manufacturing equipment, which started to turn up in F9/25, as well as a likely recovery in non-industrial applications such as medical & bio, as customer inventory adjustments conclude. However, continued increases in depreciation and R&D expenses, as well as the time required for NKTP (acquired in 2024) to turn profitable, suggest it will take considerable time to exceed F9/22's peak OP of ¥57.0bn. We forecast F9/27 OP up ¥2.9bn YoY to ¥25.4bn (consensus ¥24.4bn), driven by top-line growth not only in semiconductor equipment but also in medical & bio, and a ¥1.0bn reduction in laser-related losses. Meanwhile, we also expect higher fixed costs, including depreciation from new plant operations. Although inventory reductions are set to continue, we believe sales have bottomed and are starting to recover, which should enable utilization rates to press higher and provide underlying support for profits.

We forecast F9/26 3Q OP to decline ¥2.7bn QoQ, from ¥7.6bn to ¥4.9bn (consensus ¥5.3bn). Hamamatsu typically sees seasonal declines in 1Q and 3Q and increases in 2Q and 4Q, and so we expect a QoQ decline in 3Q. However, earnings are on a recovery trend, with OP expected to jump from ¥1.5bn in F9/25 3Q and ¥2.4bn in F9/26 1Q.

## Kyocera (6971, EW, PT ¥4,000)

We forecast F3/27 OP up ¥14.5bn YoY to ¥132.6bn (guidance ¥130.0bn, FactSet consensus ¥125.8bn). By segment, we forecast Core Components OP at ¥82.6bn (up ¥19.5bn YoY from ¥63.1bn), Electronic Components at ¥22.0bn (up ¥14.7bn from ¥7.3bn), and Solutions ¥86.0bn (down ¥17.9bn from ¥103.9bn). The decline in Solutions mainly reflects the absence of profits and gains from the divestment of SouthernCarlson (machine tools) in the prior year. We forecast F3/28 OP up ¥15.9bn YoY to ¥148.5bn (consensus ¥149.2bn), with gradual improvement across all segments. Price pass-throughs on products that could not fully reflect higher raw material costs in the prior year should also support earnings growth.

We forecast F3/27 1Q OP down ¥18.6bn QoQ to ¥28.9bn (FS consensus ¥27.4bn). The sharp QoQ decline in Solutions reflects the absence of roughly ¥17.0bn in disposal gains in the prior quarter and seasonally weaker document-related sales, which typically peak in

## 4Q and decline in 1Q.

## Taiyo Yuden (6976, UW, PT ¥12,500)

We forecast F3/27 OP up ¥25.2bn YoY to ¥45.2bn (guidance ¥30.0bn, FactSet consensus ¥40.2bn). In MLCCs, we look for sales (on a USD basis) growth of 17.1% YoY (21.0% on JPY basis), accelerating from 10.4% growth in F3/26 (8.5% in JPY), with higher utilization absorbing increases in fixed costs such as depreciation. We forecast F3/28 OP up ¥25.0bn YoY to ¥70.2bn (consensus ¥67.2bn), supported by 19.0% YoY growth in MLCC sales (both on USD and JPY basis) and continued absorption of fixed cost increases (depreciation, etc).

We forecast F3/27 1Q OP at ¥7.0bn (consensus ¥7.3bn), up ¥3.5bn QoQ from ¥3.5bn last 4Q. MLCC utilization is likely to rise to 90% from just under 85% in 4Q, supporting top-line growth.

## Murata Mfg (6981, OW, PT ¥12,500)

We expect OP to increase by ¥150bn YoY, to ¥431.9bn, in F3/27 (guidance: ¥380.0bn, FS consensus: ¥423.5bn), assuming growth of ¥111.3bn to ¥426.4bn for components and ¥38.0bn, to ¥11.5bn profit, in devices/modules. We foresee growth for components as higher depreciation and other fixed costs are absorbed by positives including ongoing

acceleration of YoY growth in MLCC sales on a US dollar basis, from +13.8% in F3/26 (+12.6% in yen) to +18.8% (+22.2% in yen), improvement in the product mix, and rising line operating rates. The growth that we foresee for devices/modules profit mainly rests with non-reoccurrence of ¥43.8bn in Resonant goodwill impairment though the business will also lack a ¥6.0bn gain on the transfer of micro-battery business. Our overall OP forecast for F3/28 is ¥624.0bn (FS: ¥579.3bn), up by ¥192.1bn YoY on gains of ¥152.9bn in components and ¥39.2bn in devices/modules. We foresee profit growth for components as a 24.0% YoY rise in sales of capacitors (MLCCs) when converted into US\$ (also +24.0% on a yen basis) and ongoing improvement in the product mix continue to absorb growth in fixed costs including depreciation; and for devices/modules with help from rising sales of high-frequency modules among other factors.

We forecast F3/27 1Q OP of ¥90.4bn (FS: ¥85.6bn), up by ¥11.5bn QoQ with components profit increasing by ¥10.8bn QoQ to ¥91.9bn, and devices/modules up by ¥0.4bn QoQ to breakeven. We expect components to see continuing growth in sales of MLCCs for AI/data centers as well as ongoing improvement in the MLCC product mix and growth in capacity utilization, and devices modules continuing improvement in battery earnings and bottoming out of sales for North American smartphones, despite non-reoccurrence of the ¥6.0bn gain on the sale of micro-battery business.

## Nichicon (6996, EW, PT ¥4,700)

We forecast OP +¥2.9bn YoY to ¥9.4bn in F3/27 (guidance: ¥8.7bn, FS consensus: ¥9.8bn), with capacitor sales advancing ¥14.6bn YoY to ¥117.3bn (guidance: ¥112.0bn) on rising demand for use in GPU servers and recovery in demand for aluminum electrolytic capacitors for autos and industrial equipment. The ¥2.6bn YoY rise that we foresee for capacitor profit to ¥7.2bn assumes positives including reviving operating rates on AEC lines and fewer losses on film capacitors for xEV use. We forecast NECST profit +¥0.3bn YoY at ¥2.2bn.

Our F3/28 OP forecast is ¥12.4bn (FS consensus: ¥12.5bn), up ¥3.0bn YoY mainly supported by continuing sales growth for large AECs for the next generation of AI GPU servers, and for conductive polymer capacitors. With added help from price-passthrough of raw material cost growth seen in F3/27, we see capacitor profit increasing ¥2.4bn YoY to ¥9.6bn.

For F3/27 1Q, we forecast OP down ¥0.9bn QoQ at ¥1.7bn (FS: ¥1.5bn), including falls each for capacitors and NECST owing to seasonally-driven sales contraction.

## Nippon Chemi-Con (6997, UW, PT ¥3,500)

Our F3/27 OP forecast of ¥7.0bn (guidance: ¥8.0bn, FS: ¥7.4bn) looks to a YoY rise of ¥3.6bn. Aluminum electrolytic capacitors made up 88% of sales in F3/26, and in F3/27 we expect a large YoY rise of ¥18.3bn in related sales as, besides benefiting from a jump in demand for large units for primary power sources with generational transition in GPU servers, we foresee steady growth in demand for automotive use and recovery in demand relating to industrial equipment. Even so, we think profit will come in short of guidance which we view as aggressive. While anticipating YoY growth in OP on factors including effects of higher sales and a better mix with gains in value-added products for servers and autos, we think profit will be below the market consensus as well as the company plan.

For F3/28, we forecast OP +¥2.1bn YoY at ¥9.1bn (FS: ¥10.0bn) as an ongoing uptrend in sales, mainly for GPU server power source and automotive applications, continues to support earnings expansion. At the same time, amid continuing severe competition with China region rivals, as with the current mid-term plan we too expect sales growth to level off in the market for commodity products for consumer applications such as PCs and household electricals. A key question will be whether effects of self-help efforts such as optimization of production locations and local sourcing of materials materialize in terms of reducing costs. In addition, the firm issued Class C/D shares on June 29 2026, and risk of added dilution, combined with the obligation to pay preferred dividends of ¥540mn/year (up to June 29 2029) and then ¥720mn/year (from June 30 2029), in our view constitute further grounds for maintaining our rating at UW.

We forecast F3/27 1Q of ¥1.2bn, down ¥0.2bn QoQ in a modest fall reflecting lower sales of all products apart from AECs and rising costs for raw materials such as aluminum foil.

Note: Consensus is based on FactSet; Source: FactSet, MS

## KOA (6999, EW, PT ¥3,000)

Our F3/27 OP forecast of ¥3.2bn looks to a YoY fall of ¥0.4bn (guidance: ¥2.8bn, FS consensus: ¥3.2bn). Our forecast of a YoY rise of ¥6.6bn in full-year sales, to ¥78.9bn, is higher than the company's (guidance: ¥77.4bn) with our outlook including widening demand for resistors for AI servers and reviving demand for use in autos and industrial equipment. We expect OP as well, though edging down YoY, to come in a little above guidance thanks to mild progress with passing on costs in prices and overshooting sales.

We forecast OP of ¥5.2bn for F3/28 (current mid-term plan target: ¥7.4bn, FS: ¥6.3bn). We continue to see high hurdles to achieving current MTP targets, and expect the pace of sales growth to slow in the mainstay automotive resistor market amid softness for BEVs and stiffer competition with Taiwanese and Chinese rivals. On the other hand we think it likely that the breadth of opportunities for the firm's business relating to AI servers – encompassing power sources, optical transceivers and SPE to name just some of its offerings – should ensure continuing expansion of AI server-related demand for value-added resistor devices, including thin-film chips and shunts, in and after F3/28.

For F3/27 1Q we forecast OP +¥0.1bn QoQ to ¥0.6bn (FS: ¥0.5bn), on the view that sales will stay flat QoQ but positive effects of reduced depreciation should cancel out the negative of rising raw material costs.

## STOCK RATINGS

MS uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). MS does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of all ratings used in MS. In addition, since MS contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read MS, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in MS is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
